**Lemma 1 (invariant measures are smooth densities):** Let $\mu$ be a $g_t$-invariant probability. Define $T_\mu\in\mathcal D'$: $T_\mu(\varphi) = \int\varphi\,d\mu$. Then $\mathcal L_XT_\mu = 0$: $\langle\mathcal L_XT_\mu,\varphi\rangle = -T_\mu(X\varphi) = -\int X\varphi\,d\mu = -\frac{d}{dt}|_{t=0}\int\varphi\circ g_t\,d\mu = -\frac{d}{dt}|_0\int\varphi\,d\mu = 0$ ✓ (invariance: $\varphi\circ g_t$ integrals constant ✓). P (with $f = 0$) ⇒ $T_\mu$ is smooth: ∃$\rho\in C^\infty$: $T_\mu(\varphi) = \int\rho\varphi\,d\omega_0$ ∀$\varphi$: i.e., $\mu = \rho\omega_0$ ✓. ✓ RIGOROUS. (Note: need $f=0$ to be a "smooth function": yes ✓.)

**Lemma 2 (density is a first-integral-like / positivity set invariant):** $\mu = \rho\omega_0$ invariant. Then for the flow: $\int\varphi\,d((g_t)_*\mu) = \int\varphi$-hmm: $(g_t)_*\mu = \mu$: in terms of densities: $(g_t)_*(\rho\omega_0) = \mu$: i.e., $\rho(g_{-t}x)\omega_0$-transported...: derive the PDE: for all test $\varphi$, $t$: $\int\varphi(g_tx)\rho(x)d\omega_0(x) = \int\varphi(y)\rho(y)d\omega_0(y)$. Differentiate at $t=0$: $\int(X\varphi)\rho\,d\omega_0 = 0$ ∀$\varphi$: ⇒ (fundamental lemma of calculus of variations) $X\rho + \rho\operatorname{div}_0X = 0$ i.e. $\operatorname{div}_0(\rho X) = 0$ ✓. Then: $\frac{d}{dt}[\rho(g_tx)e^{\int_0^t\operatorname{div}_0X(g_sx)ds}]$: compute: $\frac{d}{dt}\rho(g_tx) = (X\rho)(g_tx) = -\rho(g_tx)\operatorname{div}_0X(g_tx)$: so $\rho(g_tx) = \rho(x)\exp(-\int_0^t\operatorname{div}_0X(g_sx)ds)$ ✓✓. Hence $\rho(g_tx) = 0\iff\rho(x) = 0$ ✓: $\{\rho>0\}$ is $g_t$-invariant (both directions ✓) and open ✓. ✓ RIGOROUS.

**Lemma 3 (closed invariant sets contain open invariant subsets):** $K\ne\emptyset$ closed invariant. The restricted flow on $K$ has an invariant probability $\nu$ with $\mathrm{supp}\nu\subseteq K$ (Krylov–Bogolyubov: $\frac1T\int_0^T\varphi\circ g_t\,d\nu_0$ for any $\nu_0\in\mathcal P(K)$; limit points invariant, supported in $K$ ✓ — need $g_t(K) = K$ ✓ closedness; and to get an ERGODIC one: ergodic decomposition on the compact metric $K$ with the $\mathbb R$-action ✓ standard). By Lemma 1-2: $\nu = \rho\omega_0$, $\rho\in C^\infty$, $\rho\ge0$ (positivity: $\nu$ positive measure ⇒ $\rho\ge0$ ✓: $\int\rho\varphi\,d\omega_0\ge0$ ∀$\varphi\ge0$ ⇒ $\rho\ge0$ ✓), $\rho\not\equiv0$ (ν probability ✓), and $U_\nu := \{\rho>0\}$ open invariant nonempty ⊆ K?? — need $\{\rho>0\}\subseteq K$: $\nu(M\setminus K) = 0$: $\int_{M\setminus K}\rho\,d\omega_0 = 0$: with $\rho\ge0$ smooth and $M\setminus K$ OPEN: $\rho\equiv0$ on $M\setminus K$ ✓ (nonneg smooth function with zero integral over an open set vanishes there ✓). So $U_\nu\subseteq K$ ✓✓. ✓ RIGOROUS.

**Theorem 4 (minimality):** minimal sets exist (Zorn: intersection of chains of nonempty compact invariant sets is nonempty compact invariant ✓). Let $K$ be a minimal set: by Lemma 3 (applied with an ergodic $\nu$ on $K$): $U := \{\rho>0\}$: open invariant, $\emptyset\ne U\subseteq K$. Orbit of any $x\in U$: contained in $U$ (invariance ✓) and dense in $K$ (minimality ✓): so $K\subseteq\bar U$: with $U\subseteq K$: $\bar U = K$. Boundary $\partial U = \bar U\setminus U = K\setminus U$: closed (✓) invariant (✓: $g_t\partial U = \partial(g_tU) = \partial U$ ✓). **Theorem K (Kushnirenko [Kus74]).** Let $Y$ be a $C^\infty$ vector field on a compact manifold $N$ with flow $\Phi=(\Phi_t)_{t\in\mathbb R}$. If some $\Phi$-invariant ergodic probability measure has positive metric entropy (equivalently, if $h_{top}(\Phi_1)>0$), then there exist a smooth function $f\in C^\infty(N)$ and a distribution $u\in\mathcal D'(N)$ which is not a smooth function, such that $\mathcal L_Yu = f$ holds in the sense of distributions.

Attribution text: "This statement — that positive topological entropy forces the existence of non-smooth distributional solutions of the cohomological equation — is a classical result of A. G. Kushnirenko; it is folklore-standard in the theory of the Livšic cohomological equation, where it is the reason for which smooth solvability of $\mathcal L_Xu=f$ cannot be expected in positive entropy (see [Kus74; dlLMM86, Introduction])." — and bibliography: [Kus74] A. G. Kushnirenko, "An estimate of the entropy of smooth dynamical systems", Functional Anal. Appl. 8 (1974), 73–? (translated from Funkcional. Now Theorem 5.1 (Kushnirenko) — final formulation: 

**Theorem 5.1 (Kushnirenko).** Let $Y$ be a $C^\infty$ vector field on a compact manifold $N$ with flow $\Phi_t$. If some $\Phi$-invariant probability measure $\mu$ has positive metric entropy, $h_\mu(\Phi_1)>0$, then there exist a smooth function $f\in C^\infty(N)$ and a distribution $u\in\mathcal D'(N)$ such that $\mathcal L_Yu = f$ in $\mathcal D'(N)$, and $u$ is not smooth (i.e., not of the form $h\,\omega$ for a smooth function $h$ and any smooth density $\omega$).

Attribution: "This is the classical theorem of A. G. Kushnirenko [Kus74]: positive entropy forces non-smooth distributional solutions of the cohomological equation. ... The proof in [Kus74] is based on an ε-entropy (finite-dimensional approximation) argument; see also the discussion in [dlLMM86]. In the appendix we prove the theorem in the presence of periodic orbits or singular invariant measures, and — via Katok's closing lemma — for hyperbolic invariant measures; the general minimal uniquely ergodic case requires the full machinery of [Kus74]."

- $M$ compact connected smooth ($C^\infty$) manifold, possibly with no other assumptions; fix once and for all a smooth positive density $\omega_0$ (exists: e.g., Riemannian volume of some metric; works also if $M$ non-orientable since densities exist ✓).
- $\mathcal D'(M) := $ the topological dual of $C^\infty(M)$ (with its Fréchet topology). Since $M$ compact, test functions = $C^\infty(M)$ ✓.
- Identify $h\in C^\infty(M)$ with the distribution $[h]:\varphi\mapsto\int_Mh\varphi\,d\omega_0$. Say "$u$ is smooth" if $u = [h]$ for some $h\in C^\infty(M)$. (Remark: this notion doesn't depend on $\omega_0$ in the sense that smoothness-of-the-distribution is equivalent for any two choices ✓: if $[h]_{\omega_0} = [h']_{\omega_0'}$ then $h' = h\cdot\omega_0/\omega_0'$ smooth ✓.)
- Define $\mathcal L_X:\mathcal D'\to\mathcal D'$: $\langle\mathcal L_Xu,\varphi\rangle := -\langle u, X\varphi\rangle$. Note $\mathcal L_X[h] = [\,Xh + h\,\operatorname{div}_{\omega_0}X\,]$ — proof by parts. (So for the smooth class, $\mathcal L_X$ is the "divergence-form" derivative; if $\operatorname{div}_{\omega_0}X = 0$, it's just $X$.)

**Lemma 2.1:** For $h\in C^\infty(M)$: $\mathcal L_X[h] = [Xh + h\,\operatorname{div}_0X]$. Proof: $\langle\mathcal L_X[h],\varphi\rangle = -\int hX\varphi\,d\omega_0$. By the divergence theorem applied to the field $h\varphi X$: $0 = \int\operatorname{div}_0(h\varphi X)\,d\omega_0 = \int(X(h\varphi) + h\varphi\operatorname{div}_0X)\,d\omega_0 = \int(\varphi Xh + hX\varphi + h\varphi\operatorname{div}_0X)\,d\omega_0$. So $-\int hX\varphi\,d\omega_0 = \int\varphi(Xh + h\operatorname{div}_0X)\,d\omega_0$ ✓.

**Lemma 2.3 (invariant measure):** $\mu$ probability, $(g_t)_*\mu = \mu$ ∀t. Then ∀$\varphi$, $t\mapsto\int\varphi\circ g_t\,d\mu$ is constant ⇒ $\int X\varphi\,d\mu = 0$ ⇒ $\langle\mathcal L_XT_\mu,\varphi\rangle = 0$ ✓.

**Lemma 3.1:** (H) ⇒ for every invariant probability $\mu$: ∃$\rho\in C^\infty(M)$, $\rho\ge0$, with $\mu = \rho\omega_0$; moreover $\operatorname{div}_0(\rho X)\equiv0$ and hence $\rho(g_tx) = \rho(x)\exp(-\int_0^t(\operatorname{div}_0X)(g_sx)ds)$; consequently $\{\rho>0\}$ is an open $g_t$-invariant set and $\rho$ vanishes on its complement. Proof: Lemma 2.3 + (H) with $f=0$ gives $T_\mu = [\rho]$, i.e. $\int\varphi\,d\mu = \int\rho\varphi\,d\omega_0$ ∀φ ⇒ $\mu = \rho\omega_0$ (measures equal ⇒ densities equal a.e.; smoothness fine). Positivity: for $\varphi\ge0$: $\int\rho\varphi\,d\omega_0 = \int\varphi\,d\mu\ge0$ ⇒ $\rho\ge0$ (a nonnegative distributional density: if $\rho(x_0)<0$ then a small nonneg test function around $x_0$ gives negative ⇒ contradiction ✓). Then $\mathcal L_XT_\mu = 0 = \mathcal L_X[\rho] = [X\rho+\rho\operatorname{div}_0X]$ ⇒ $X\rho = -\rho\operatorname{div}_0X$ pointwise (a smooth function whose pairing with all test functions vanishes is 0 ✓). ODE: $\frac{d}{dt}\rho(g_tx) = (X\rho)(g_tx) = -\rho(g_tx)(\operatorname{div}_0X)(g_tx)$ ⇒ solve ✓. Invariance of positivity set: $\rho(g_tx) = 0\iff\rho(x) = 0$ ✓. Openness ✓.

**Proposition 3.2:** (H) ⇒ every nonempty closed $g_\bullet$-invariant $K\subseteq M$ contains a nonempty open $g_\bullet$-invariant subset. Proof: pick $\nu_0\in\mathcal P(K)$; $\nu_T := \frac1T\int_0^T(g_t)_*\nu_0dt$; any weak-* limit $\nu$ (T_j→∞) is $g_\bullet$-invariant (standard: shift argument ✓ I'll include it) and supported in $K$ ✓. Lemma 3.1: $\nu = \rho\omega_0$, $U := \{\rho>0\}$: open, invariant; and $\nu(M\setminus K) = 0$ with $M\setminus K$ open and $\rho\ge0$ smooth ⇒ $\rho|_{M\setminus K}\equiv0$ ⇒ $U\subseteq K$ ✓ nonempty since $\rho\not\equiv0$ ✓.

**Theorem 3.3 (minimality):** (H) ⇒ either $M\cong S^1$ and $M$ is a single periodic orbit, or the flow is minimal.
